Настоящий том является одним из 13 томов издания "Труды 
XIII 
Международного конгресса по истории науки", который проходил в Москве с 18 по 24 августа 1971 г. В этом томе содержатся доклады, прочитанные на секциях "История и перспективы развития системного 
подхода и обшей теории систем" и "История организации научных исследований". Они посвящены изучению закономерностей развития системного подхода и общей теории систем и истории организации научных исследований.

## Страница 7

П.К. Ано х ин (СССР)
ВОЗНИКНОВЕНИЕ И ПЕРСПЕКТИВЫ РАЗВИТИЯ ТЕОРИИ
ФУНКЦИОНАЛЬНОЙ СИСТЕМЫ
Представление о функциональной системе как о единице интегративной деятельности организма возникло в нашей лаборатории в связи с 
необходимостью объяснить механизмы компенсации нарушенных функций 
(П.Анохин, 1933, 1935). Это обстоятельство и определило в дальнейшем 
особенности наших поисков общих законов функционирования реальных 
биологических систем.
Первым большим достижением на этом пути было открытие постоянного обратного влияния со стороны 
органов действия и конечных результатов действия на формирование функциональных систем в целом. 
Это обратное регулирующее влияние на систему со стороны параметров 
полезного результата системы было названо нами "санкционирующей" 
или "обратной афферентацией" (П. Анохин, 1935, 1939, 1947).
На протяжении ряда лет (1935-1970) были выработаны: а) понятие 
системы, б) строгие правила формирования системы и в) механизмы 
иерархизации функциональных систем.
Системообразующим фактором признан полезный результат системы, 
под влиянием которого функциональная система находится с самого 
начала формирования.
Была разработана конкретная операциональная архитектоника системы, которая в силу своей специфики и физиологической конкретности 
стала основным фактором тонкого аналитического исследования свойств 
системы до молекулярного уровня включительно.
В результате многочисленных исследований был раскрыт постоянно 
действующий в пределах системы аппарат кодирования ведущих параметров будущего, еще не полученного результату. Этот аппарат, прогнозируя важнейшие свойства полезного результата, служит и аппаратом 
сличения прогнозируемого и реального результатов по параметрам обратной афферентации.
В силу того, что центральным организующим фактором функциональной системы является полезный результат системы, все разработанные 
закономерности функциональной системы и ее архитектоника оказались 
целиком переносимыми и обязательными для всех систем с полезным 
результатом (машинные, общественные, промышленные и другие организации). Этим самым функциональная система становится основной 
матрицей для поиска условий прогрессивного развития системных организаций ("малых" и "больших" систем).
Перспективы развития теории функциональных систем характеризуются принципиальным переломом в использовании их закономерностей: 
из объекта исследования функциональная система превращается в методологический инструмент постановки новых проблем исследования и 
становится ключом к прогрессивному использованию уже накопленного 
наукой материала.
В докладе даны примеры решающих и принципиальных изменений 
в трактовке традиционных проблем биологии, физиологии и кибернетики.

L’ACTIVITE SCIENTIFIQUE COM M E F A C T E U R
DE S T R U C T U R A T IO N S O C IA L E
Depuis 1?expansion de la révolution scientifique-technologique, 1!objet de la sociologie de la science s'est élargi: 
à coté du problème classique du conditionnement social de 
l'activité scientifique, il s'impose le thème de l'influence 
exercée par celle-ci sur le développement social. Il y a de 
nombreuses études pertinentes au sujet du role important de la 
science dans la vie contemporaine. Tout de meme, l'évaluation 
théorique et la conceptualisation de cette réalité ne dépasse 
pas, en général, le langage intuitif dans lequel P.Auger constate, par exemple, que la science de nos jours a atteint 
"le rang d'un grand facteur social".
On ne doit pas etre marxiste pour reconnaître qu'une des 
plus importantes contributions à la détermination conceptuelle 
de la fonction sociale de la science reste, jusqu'à présent, la 
définition donnée par K.Marx qui la considère force productive 
immédiate (directe). Quoique cette indication reste un élément essentiel pour une théorie sociologique sur l'influence 
exercée par l'activité scientifique sur le développement social 
les problèmes soulevés par l'essor scientifique actuel obligent 
à créer de nouveaux concepts en vue de l'élaboration d'une 
théorie de Inaction inverse (feedback) du développement de la 
science sur le développement social.
En vue de l'interprétation convenable du matériel empirique immense qui se rapporte à ce que vaguement on nomme 
"l'influence de la science sur la société", nous introduisons
7


## Страница 9

deux concepts: "fonction sociogonique" et "structure sociogonique "• Par l’intermède de ceux-ci, nous essaierons de définir, plus tard, l’action inverse de la science sur la société.
Le terme de "sociogonie" dérive du radical "socio-" 
(social, ce qui se rapporte à la société, sociétal) et du 
élin "gonos" (génération, générateur).
Nous comprendrons par fonction sociogonique la propriété 
inhérente à toute pratique sociale (ou activité humaine collective) de générer, par son propre déroulement, de nouvelles structures sociales, de transformer celles qui existent, 
et, en général, d'accomplir le rôle de facteur structurant 
des processus sociaux: a) des systèmes sociaux globaux ^sociétés, états, communités nationales...), b) de leurs sous-systèmes (les sous-systèmes de l’économie, de l’enseignement. ••), ainsi que de c) soi-meme (1'autoréfléxivité de 
l'activité ou de la pratique sociale).
A son tour, la structure de sociogonie constitue un 
groupe relativement invariant de facteurs dynamiques (une 
structure de génération), par laquelle se réalise une certaine 
fonction sociogonique.
Il est intuitivement évident que l’activité scientifique 
"produit" d’un coté des résultats cognitifs (connaissances,...) 
opérationnels (méthodes, •••), mais en meme temps, comme toute 
pratique, elle génère, d'un autre coté, des processus sociaux de structuration, certaines relations dans le cadre du 
système propre (autostructuration), ainsi que dans le cadre 
du système social surordoné ou de ses sous-systèmes.
L'action inverse de l'activité scientifique sur le système social global, dont elle constitue le sous-système ( et 
dépend de celui-ci), se manifeste, en principal, par l’action 
de structuration sociale qu’elle exerce par 1) la contribution
8


## Страница 10

à la "production" de nouvelles structures (ou systèmes) sociaux globaux, 2) la stabilisation ou désorganisation de certaines structures, respectivement systèmes sociaux globaux ou de 
leurs sous-sy3tèmes, 3) 1*auto-"production" ou autostabilisation (respectivement, lfautodésorganisation) de sa propre structure ou de son propre système (en tant que sous-système d'un 
système social global).
On distingue deux sous-classes de la fonction sociogonique de 1*activité scientifique:
I# La fonction macrosociogonique réside dans son action 
de générer des structures, de structurer ou restructurer (soit 
désorganiser) le système social, comme tout, réalisée en principal par 1*intermède de 1*action exercée comme force productive immédiate.
II. La fonction microsociogonique de 1*activité scientifique consiste dans son action de structurer et restructurer ses 
propres formes de déroulement, en commençant par la formation 
du sous-système social "le monde de la science" (the world of 
science), passant par différentes institutionalisations de la 
recherche et de sa direction, jusqu*à 1*apparition et le développement des microstructures spécifiques: l'équipe (ou le 
collectif) de recherche (the collective researcher).
Dans cette phase de l'investigation, nous pouvons formuler 
seulement comme une hypothèse extrêmement plausible qu'une 
étude sur la corrélation qui s'établit entre les caractéristiques de la courbe de croissance de l'information, du nombre 
des chercheurs et des publications existentes, entre les courbes de la croissance des investitions dans la science et celles 
du revenu national (ou du produit national brut), de la gamme 
de produits, de l'éventail des professions, de la productivité, 
ainsi que sur les caractéristiques structurelés de la pratique, 
de la pepulation, etc. puisse attester Inexistence vérifiable

Les formes variées d’organisation, le nombre optimale 
des unités scientifiques et leur degré d'efficience (économique, scientifique, etc*) expriment, à leur tour, l’intensité

Dans ce cadre général, esquissé seulement dans notre communication, on pourrait formuler une hypothèse concernant la 
loi de la croissance continuelle de la fonction sociogonique 
de l ’activité scientifique.
La "production" de l’information scientifique génère avec 
nécéssité, comme on l’a vu, une fonction détérminée, la "production" de structures et structurations sociales spécifiques.
D’ici, il suit: si la production de l’information scientifique possède un caractère de loi, alors la production de 
structures et structurations sociales correspondantes - comm 
fonction de la première - possède, elle aussi, un caractère 
de loi. Et comm pour la production de l’information scientifique on a prouvé (les relation de De Solia Price) que sa 
croissance continuelle a un caractère de loi, l'hypothèse 
que la croissance continuelle de la fonction sociogonique 
de l’activité scientifique a un caractère de loi peut être 
considérée extrêmement plausible.
Enfin, au niveau de l’unité de base du "monde de la 
science", au niveau de l'équipe de recherche - le chercheur 
collectif - se manifeste, ainsi que sur l’ensemble de ce sous-système spécialisé, une structure dynamique, sociogonique fondamentale: la structure de coopération*
L’existence de cette structure constitutive de la pratique scientifique contemporaine et de ses formes d’organisation 
optimales met en évidence une grave contradiction, et, en 
meme temps, un sériuex problème de prognose sociale*

scientifique et de l’utilisation sociale de se résultats proviennent

Scope of the Problem
Eighty percent of the world’s population is concentrated on fifteen percent of the land. In I960 the world's urban 
population was 33*1% of its population, and in 1970 - 37.1%* 
Prospective urban populations for I960, 1990 and 2000 cure 
respectively 41*2%, 46% and 36%. It is obvious that we must 
become ever more concerned about relatively high, densely
12


## Страница 14

concentrated agglomeration and settlement9 Further, in a typical metropolitan area, it has been estimated there are some 
20,000 decisions affecting development each year, plus an 
additional 20,000 decisions to alter and improve property.
These areas "present clear case histories of . »• contemporary society's most up-to-date ills - racial, social, 
educational, economic, technological and aesthetic - in one 
complex, overwhelming, solution-defying package"1^. The "package" quality of these problems gives rise to the need to 
view their solution in terms of a system and simplifying models.
Simplification and more rigorous methods of decision-making with electronic aid become essential in sensing and 
processing information. While effective decisions require 
recognition of realities, reality cannot be fully understood 
in its totality through sense perception alone. Information 
overload increases with each sensor man develops to assist 
him in grasping "facts".
However, despite increased ability to sense the real 
world and process data inputs, man will only be able to sense 
the real world in bits and pieces, so rich is the river of 
data to be observed. It is, therefore, necessary to develop 
more generalized, but useful, principles for organizing data 
points. The generalizations become scales for weighing or 
measuring realities. Laws and principles subject to verification by specific rules of scientific observations then are 
evolved. In the development of these generalizations General 
Systems hesearch is most important.
Importance of General Systems Research in Societal Problem
Solutions to the complex of systems require "orchestration" of disciplines - of life and natural scientists working 
together with social scientists. Systems know little of boundaries.
13

 For systems are transdiscipUnary• hence, systems 
research is useful in public decision-making.
An even more important reason for applications of general 
systems is that resources are not infinite. Research in systems can help us make the most of such resources as are avai-

lable. 
It is likely that "more difficult choices, greater 
efficiency, increased productivity and better management will 
have to be the way of life in the future"*^ *
To help answer how to cope with a world of such decision-making, the system approach then affords some opportunity# 
Interface and Resting of Urban Systems in General Systems 
System analysis, system design and system management 
depend on analogy expressed in generally applicable models# 
General systems absorb contributions of other fields!
(a) cybernetics, the science of communications 
controls; operations research, the application of mathematical modeling to researching 
operations and programs;
(b) information theory dealingwith the mathematical modeling of probability distributions 
for clearing channels of communication of 
random noise; organization theory
Systems as a discipline has obtained from these fields 
generalizations relating to process. Procedures f^r identifying and studying any process as a system include^'*
(1) determination of the anatomy or geography 0f 
the phenomena, so as to ascertain the parameters of relevancy;
(2) determination of the simpliest dynamics of 
the system, that is, the predetermined and 
necessary changes. This is the clockwork of 
the system;


## Страница 16

(3) determination of the control mechanisms which 
govern the system, i*e*, the cybernetics of 
the system.
Describing the transmission, interpretation and transformation of information helps locate Mcontrollers*M If a 
system contains its own regulators, it is closed-loop system* 
But it may be that governors come from an outside environment 
This is an open-system* The closed-loop system is self-maintaining* The open system is fed by outside inputs preventing 
entropy and generating organization. Urban systems are open, 
depend, in some significant measure, on government regulators 
and tend toward equifinality, not equilibria* The regulators 
change depending on changing objectives and changing technological, physical, social and economic elements and systems 
nested in environments constituting the ecology within which 
the system operates**\ Differentiating factors in urban systems are products of the unique stress placed upon each system by its particular environment in terms of the number, 
identity and magnitude of inputs* The governmental regulators of urban systems become embodied in forming-of-response 
processors characterized in the structure established precedent* These allow a given urban system to exhibit maintenan 
ce functions, regulation and control functions, goal-seeking 
feedback and 6oal-changing feedback* as an aspect of maintenance, the legal structure and established institutionalized precedents display plasticity and elasticity* New institutions may be added to urban systems but historical programming institutions persist and adapt* They may be absorbed 
or they may wither, but they rarely disappear.
Urban systems involve human actors* Hence, high mobility 
self-awareness, multi-dimensional feedback, and consideration 
as to image creation, pattern recognition, and motive are


## Страница 17

important. The individual system is embedded in psychological 
space which constitute elements in the social system. But 
social organization is a larger scale system interfacing with 
human systems. Social organization generates political organization which, in turn, generate the control mechanisms.
Hence, differential systems nested in each other characterize urban systems. There are, then, many problems involved 
in articulating the organization of each urban system around 
the elements we have tried to identify. While it is probably 
true tnat any articulated urban system composed of its component subsystems will contain self-organizing, maintaining 
and regulatory functions, one of the elements will always be 
governmental policies which change and act as important regulators processed through law and precedent. All these elements must be jarticulated, quantified, and successfully related to each other.
The Systems Approach. Data Processing, and Urban Systems 
Designing
The system approach requires identification and quantification of elements and system states. To establish relationships, knowledge of system dynamics is necessary. Data 
and information flow are the life blood of systems. Computer 
technology is, therefore, necessary to speed up and order 
data. But even with computer aid, urban system complexity 
and difficulties of measuring standards and performance challenge.
Progress in urban systems design has been slow. The environment within which urban systems engineering operates is 
not only physical, but man-made law, and regulation, and historically constrained habit and custom. Further, there are 
externalities and spill-overs which make measurement difficult. The time-dimension in systems dynamics differ with di/.O'tWTI
16

ffering subsystems. The telos or objectives of given subsystems 
differ and bombard total system output. The scale of subsystems offers challenging problems also. As a result of these 
difficulties, suboptimization may not be relevant. The terms 
"satisfising" or "subutoptian** may be more descriptive of the 
equifinality states characterizing urban systems. Such may 
constitute the basis of the optima we can express.
Conclusion 
j
The technically trained system analyst generally has 
tended to exclude from consideration the "non-mathematisizable? 
the non-measurable, the ♦•human*1 and value considerations in 
complex systems. These must now be specifically included in 
urban system analysis and design. For even systems of mass 
transit, housing construction, and environmental pollution 
control - all of which are related - contain important behavioral elements found in individual and social/psychological 
behavior, reaction, motivation and values. These constrain 
purely technological elements. The field of systems, man and 
cybernetics, then, constitutes a new worthwhile basis for 
evoking solutions to problems of urban life, while more research is needed, there is great opportunity in these approaches to urban problem solving.

P R O G R E S S IN G E N E R A L S Y S T E M T H E O R Y
The notion of "system” has gained central importance in 
contemporary science, society and life. In many fields of 
endeavor, the necessity of a ”systems approach” or "systems 
thinking” is emphasized, new professions called "systems 
engineer”, "systems analyst” or the like came into being, 
and there can be little doubt that this concept marks a genuine, necessary and consequential development in science 
and world view.
The present writer may be known for his work in general 
systems theory and as one of the founders of the "Society 
for General Systems Research.” It was in the late twenties 
when I emphasezed, under the title of "organismic biology,” 
the necessity of regarding the living organism as an "organized system,” and defined "the fundamental task of biology as 
discovery of the laws of biological systems at all levels 
of organization.” This trend arising in biology and other 
disciplines led me to conceive the idea of general system 
theory (introduced in the 1930s and 40s), that is, an interdisciplinary doctrine "elaborating principles and models 
that apply to systems in general, irrespective of their particular
18


## Страница 20

 kind, elements, and ’forces* involved." Although 
of different origin, parallels of the principles of general 
systems theory ana aialectical materialism are easily seen.
Classical science in its diverse disciplines, be it 
chemistry, biology, psychology or the social sciences, tried 
to isolate the elements of the observed universe - chemical 
compounds and enzymes, cells, elementary sensations, freely 
competing individuals, as the case may be - expecting that 
by putting them together again conceptually or experimentally, 
the whole or system - cell, mind, society - would result and 
be intelligible. How we have learned that for an understanding not only the elements but their interrelations as well 
are required: say, the interplay of enzymes in a cell, of 
many mental processes conscious and unconscious, the structure and dynamics of social systems and so forth. This requires exploration of the many systems in our observed universe in their own right and specificities. Moreover, it 
turns out that there are correspondences or isomorphisms 
in certain general aspects of "systems." This is the domain 
of general systems theory, xnaeed, such parallelisms or 
isomorphisms appear - sometimes surprisingly - in otherwise 
totally different "systems." General systems theory, then, 
is scientific exploration of "wholes" and "wholeness" which, 
not so long ago, were considered to be metaphysical notions 
transcending the boundaries of science. Hierarchic structure, stability, teleology, differentiation, approach to and 
maintenance of steady states, goal-directedness - these 
are a few of such general-system properties; and novel conceptions and mathematical fields have developed to deal 
with them: dynamical system theory, automata theory, system 
analysis by set, net, graph theory and others. This interdisciplinary endeavor of elaborating principles and models
19


## Страница 21

applying to Msystems" in general, at the same time provides 
a possible approach toward unification of science*
This was paralleled by developments in technology which, 
in cybernetics, control engineering, computer work, etc., 
similarly lea to models ana principles of an interaisciplinary nature, applying to generalized "systems" or classes of 
systems irrespective of their realization as mechanical, electric, biological, social, etc*, systems. N.Wiener*8 "Cybernetics" as "study of communication and control" was pioneering, and here too a crop of new approaches and theories - 
information, circuit, decision, game theory and others - 
developed* Modern technology and society have become so complex that traditional ways and means are not sufficient anymore but approaches of a "holistic" (or systems) and generalist nature have to be introduced* Innumerable problems 
in industry, commerce, politics, etc*, ask for a "systems 
approach" in automation, computerization, etc*, nandling 
interacting systems of a complexity unamenable to classical 
methods of mathematics* Systems of many levels ask for scientific controls ecosystems the disturbance of which results 
in pressing problems like pollution; formal organizations 
like a bureaucracy or an army; the grave problems appearing 
in socio-economic systems, in international relations, 
politics and deterrence* Irrespective of the questions of 
how far scientific understanding (contrasted to the admission of Irrationality in cultural and historical events) 
is possible, and to what extent scientific control is desirable, there can be no dispute that these are essentially 
"system" problems, that is, problems of interrelations of 
a great number of "variables."
Finally, there is "systems philosophy*" The concept of 
"system" constitutes a new "paradigm", to use Thomas Kuhn*s
20


## Страница 22

expression in his brilliant exposition of the MStructure of 
Scientific Kevolutions” ; or a "new philosophy of nature” 
as I myself put it, contrasting the blind laws of nature 
of the mechanistic world view and the world process as a 
Snakespearean tale told by an idiot, with an organiemic outlook of the "world as a great organization.”
Like Caesar's Gaul, it naturally divides into three 
parts. First, we must find out the "nature of the beast”.
This is systems ontology - what is meant by "system” , and 
how systems are realized in reality at the various levels 
of tne world of observation* Secondly, there is systems 
epistemology. Having been educated in the philosophical tradition later called the Vienna Circle, I may perhaps hint 
at the change that has taken place. The epistemology (and 
metaphysics) of logical positivism was determined by the 
ideas of physicalism, atomism, and the "camera theory” of 
knowledge. These, in view of present-day knowledge, are 
quite simply obsolete. Instead of physicalism and reductionists, the problems and modes of thought occurring in the 
biological, behavioral and social sciences should equally 
be taken into account. Compared to the analytical procedure 
of classic science, with linear causality connecting two 
variables as basic category, the investigation of organized 
wholes of many variables requires new categories of interaction, transaction, teleology, etc., with many problems arising in epistemology, mathematical models and techniques. 
Furthermore, knowledge is not a simple approximation to 
"truth" or "reality"; it is an interaction between knower 
and known, so depending on a multiplicity of factors of social, biological, cultural, linguistic, etc., nature. This 
leads to a "perspective philosophy" for which physics, fully 
acknowledging its achievements in its own and related fields.
21


## Страница 23

is not a monopolistic way of knowledge. Against reductionism 
and theories declaring that reality is "nothing but" (a heap 
of physical particles, genes, reflexes, drives or whatever 
the case may be), we see science as one of the "perspectives" 
man, with his social, biological, cultural and linguistic 
endowment and bondage, has created to deal with the universe 
he is "thrown in", or rather to which he is adapted owing 
to evolution and history.
The third part of systems philosophy obviously will be 
concerned with the relations of man and world, or what is 
termed "values" in philosophical parlance. If reality ia a 
hierarchy of organized wholes, the image of man will be different from what it is in a world of pnysical particles as 
ultimate and only "true" reality governed by chance events. 
Rather, the world of symbols, values, social entities and 
cultures is something very "real”; and its embeddness in a 
cosmic order of hierarchies is apt to bridge the opposition 
of C.P.Snow’s "Two Cultures" of science and the humanities, 
technology and history, natural and social sciences or in 
whatever way the antithesis is formulated1^.

И.В. Б л а у б е р г (СССР)
РАЗВИТИЕ СИСТЕМНЫХ ИССЛЕДОВАНИЙ В СОВЕТСКОМ СОЮЗЕ
Системные исследования в нашей стране представляют в настоящее 
время довольно обширную и разветвленную область научного знания. 
Внутри этой области существуют две вполне специализированные сферы - теоретико-методологическая и прикладная. Однако рамки настоящего сообщения не позволяют обстоятельно охарактеризовать всю совокупность этих исследований. Поэтому я затрону лишь те работы, которые связаны с выявлением роли специфически системных познавательных средств в процессе функционирования научного знания. При 
этом я не буду касаться широкой сферы приложений системного подхода в специальных научных и технических дисциплинах, если соответствующие работы не затрагивают непосредственно методологическую 
проблематику.
Разработка общих принципов системно-структурного исследования 
является одной из важных традиций отечественной науки. Глубокие 
системные : идеи были сформулированы выдающимся русским кристаллографом Е.С. Федоровым еще в последней четверти XIX в. Врач и 
физиолог Н.А. Белов при разработке учения о внутренней секреции органов 
и тканей выдвинул в 1911 г. принцип отрицательной обратной 
связи, имеющий, как это показали более поздние работы по кибернетике, 
не только биологическое значение. В определенной форме эти идеи получили дальнейшее развитие в тектологии А.А. Богданова, в которой 
была сделана попытка сформулировать общие структурные принципы 
организации [13].
В дальнейшем шел постепенный процесс накопления системных идей 
в различных областях знания, причем характерно, что он происходил 
во многом независимо в различных естественных науках и в философии. Системные идеи развивались такими советскими учеными, как 
Л.С. Выготский (психология), И.И. Шмальгаузен (биология), Н.А.Бернштейн (физиология высшей нервной деятельности), В.И.Вернадский 
(учение о биосфере), В.Н. Сукачев ( биогеоценология) и др. П.К. Анохин еще в начале 30-х годов сформулировал представление о функциональной системе, которое затем было значительно обогащено в ходе 
экспериментальных и теоретических исследований.
С другой стороны, в философской литературе в середине и в конце 
50-х годов особое внимание было обращено на философские и логические аспекты исследования сложных развивающихся объектов, причем 
исходным пунктом послужили прежде всего методологические принципы, 
сформулированные в "Капитале" К.Маркса. В этой области особый интерес представляют работы Э.В. Ильенкова (метод восхождения от 
абстрактного к конкретному как один из способов изучения сложного 
объекта), Б.А. Грушина (логические способы и приемы исследования 
систем, обладающих собственной историей), А.А. Зиновьева (логика 
исследований связей), М.К. Мамардашвили (процессы анализа и синтеза) [ 7 ] , [ 5 ] , [ 6 ] , [10].

## Страница 25

Позже появляется цикл работ по проблеме целостности, послуживший 
своего рода введением в философско-методологическую проблематику 
системных исследований (см., в частности, [1 ], [2 ], [11], [14]).
Первыми работами в нашей философской литературе, посвященными 
собственно системной проблематике, явились статьи В.И. Кремянского 
[8 ], В.А. Лекторского и В.Н. Садовского [9 ]. 
Нельзя не упомянуть 
в этой связи и работу семинара по вопросам методологии системно-структурного исследования, который был организован философской секцией Совета по комплексной проблеме "Кибернетика" при Президиуме 
АН СССР (обзор работы семинара см. в [12]).
Системные течения в естествознании, обществоведении, технических 
науках и философии слились в единый поток собственно системного 
движения в нашей стране сравнительно недавно, всего лишь несколько 
лет назад. Наиболее характерная черта этого процесса - равноправное 
и плодотворное участие представителей разных специальностей (биологов, психологов, математиков, философов, логиков, экономистов, лингвистов, социологов, техников и т. п.) в выработке единого подхода, 
единого языка системного исследования. И пусть результаты пока не 
столь заметны, все же сам факт такого объединения усилий, происходящего во многом спонтанно, является весьма симптоматичным. Он 
говорит о фундаментальных сдвигах в стиле научного мышления, имеющих не только и не столько узко-практический, частнонаучный, сколько методологический характер, содействуя выдвижению новых исследовательских проблем. Именно в этом я и вижу главное значение системного подхода.
К настоящему времени у нас сложились системные группы и лаборатории в ряде научно-исследовательских и высших учебных заведений. 
Они пока невелики по размерам, но уже имеют определенную специализацию, свой "профиль". К их числу относятся группы в Одесском 
государственном университете ( построение логико-математического аппарата описания системных объектов), в Институте истории естествознания и техники АН СССР (исследование этапов становления системного подхода в современной науке, разработка его философской и 
логико-матодологической проблематики, исследование статуса общей 
теории систем, изучение возможностей и путей применения системного 
подхода к исследованию науки как системы особого рода). Кроме того, 
группы и отдельные исследователи работают в Институте философии и 
права АН Армянской 
ССР, в институтах философии АН СССР и АН 
УССР, в научных учреждениях Москвы, Ленинграда, Новосибирска, Севастополя и других городов ([3 ], [4 ]).
Помимо монографий, сборников и статей в периодической печати, 
с 1969 г. издательство "Наука" выпускает ежегодник 
"Системные исследования", подготавливаемый сектором системных исследований науки Института истории естествознания и техники АН СССР.

M u c k l e y ( U S A )
TH E S Y S T E M S A P P R O A C H IN SO C IO L O G Y
I can only try to indicate briefly here two general directions in which system theory in Sociology has seemed to be 
moving, or needs to move* There is no question that very recently, social system analysis in the USA has shifted its framework, or "paradigm," from the more static, structural-functional or equilibrial focus on stability and consensus, to a 
more modern, non-Parsonian, system-theoretic perspective emphasizing the more transactional or dialectic nature of society
25


## Страница 27

 as immanently conflictful and changing. However, little 
has been done yet in the West to overcome the massive inertia 
of the old paradigm, though significant work has begun to 
appear* From what 1 understand of the high volume of system 
theorizing in the USSfi1^ the systems perspective would seem 
to be encouraged partly because it is consistent with dialectic and materialist principles shared by many scientists there.
In the rest of this paper I will discuss the two areas 
of development mentioned above in terms of, first, macrosociology, ana secondly, microsociology.
I. 
The systems view suggests a closer look at social control and planning as a cybernetic process of emergency, or 
morphogenesis. Goals, plans, and power and authority arrangements develop and change in an ongoing process of action and 
feedback transaction. A major implication is that final goals 
or societal ejid structures cannot be predetermined or built 
into the system once and for all on penalty of a rigidifying 
lack of adaptibility - the ability to restructure the system 
as a function of the information coming in from changing environmental and internal conditions stemming partly from the 
very process of planning and goalseeking itself. This capability is the genius of modern systems of regulation, and 
seems to underlie the basic regulatory systems of nature.
In the West, the study of societal regulation and control has traditionally been left to the discipline of political science, but the failure of the latter has typically been 
its normal separation of the "political" from the social and 
economic. A systems view demands the breakdown of such artificial barriers.
The need is to continue to develop a holistic conceptual 
framework for social systems that goes well beyond the now 
weak recognition that the parts of a system are related in
26


## Страница 28

some way to one another. In making a start In this direction, 
my colleague P.Pergler and I have begun from an explicit epistemology and ontology and have attempted to develop operationally and progressively more complex ways in which elements may be related to one another, building on each definition to reach a conceptualization of societal regulation 
and control* Thus, the most general expression of the relation 
between elements is that of "association." The question it 
is based on asks, Poes knowledge of the input tell us anything about the output (or vice-versa)? Next is the relation 
of "causation,” which is based on the question, Poes manipulation of the input produce a change in the output (but not 
vice-versa)? A significant increase in complexity is represented by the next concept, that of "control." It goes beyond 
causation by asking, Poes the output tend to approach a goal 
state as a function of the unput? More complex still is the 
notion of "regulation": Are there alternative means for the 
achievement of the same goal for different states ojf a disturbance? Beyond these concepts we distinguish MHomostasis" 
(Does the regulation improve the general performance of the 
system?), "adaptive regulation" (Is there a change in the 
characteristic of the regulator that Improves the regulation?), 
and similarly with "morphostasis" and "morphogenesis"*
The task now becomes that of applying such concepts to 
problems of social system regulation and control in an operationally clear manner* Not much advance can be expected 
here until we have developed further the conceptual tools to 
specify the social system of a dynamic whole - mapping out 
its major structures, its processes unfolding through time, 
its information processing nets and decision centers, etc.
Then we can ask such questions as: What does a democratic 
social system look like? how may it be fully reached and maintained?
27


## Страница 29

 If historical processes of the past have been mainly 
morphostatic or violently disruptive, how may they be made 
more morphogenic and self-regulating in a less disruptive way?
Though these are ambitious questions, I believe the systems approach promises the way to some answers•
II. Although the utlimate goal for sociology lies at 
the level of social system structure and process, this requires a better understanding of the underlying micro-processes. There is much current interest in such problems as 
the "social construction of reality,*1 group interpretations 
or definitions of the situation, and the nature and role of 
language in such constructions. Thus it becomes important to 
focus on the process of knowledge generation, and hence on 
the problem of epistemology as basic to these undertakings.
I have recently attempted to map out a systems model 
of the epistemological process, and will summarize some of 
the main ideas of this work2\ The emphasis is on the epistemological process as (not unreasonably) an information processing system. The latter is seen as basically a flow of 
invariant structure across substantive transformations, a 
"message” being a mapping of such structure between source 
and destination and across one or more transformations. In 
essence, our model of the epistemological process pictures 
a flow of information in this sense from the physical and 
social environment through the various transformati ons or 
codings of the human sensory, cognitive, linguistic and other 
mental or neurophysiological mechanisms, to the decision-making and consequent motor output apparatuses, and thus to 
actions or behaviors that constitute transactions back on 
aspects of the physical and social environment again - often 
changing the latter and hence the nature of its later inputs 
into the system.
28


## Страница 30

Prom a history of science point of view, this model 
allows us to trace out the main philosophical positions in 
epistemology - Humean empiricism, idealism, phenomenalism 
and phenomenology, the logical atomism of Russell and Wittgenstein, and logical positivism - and to show exactly in 
what way they were all only partial models of the total epistemological system and thus inadequate despite an element of 
truth in each*
Some of the main points I have tried to make with this 
model can only be summarized here as follows:
1* Adequate epistemological analysis must focus on the 
total system as a complex on-going whole. The information selection, transformations, or codings that occur at any point 
in the system depend not only on prior events and processes 
in the system, but on feedbacks from later points. A well-known example is the fact that the information attended to or 
selected for processing during activity is continually changing as a function of the on-going intentions, decisions, and 
actions of the individual. Perception, as well as conception, 
is at best a continual sampling out of the extensive potential 
informational cues available in the environment.
2. 
Thus the total system, when operating fully, is what 
I have referred to elsewhere as a transactional system, with 
morphogenic (structure-changing; as well as morphostatic 
(structure-preserving) capabilities. What this means, among 
other things, is that knowledge is not passively and finally 
given merely through information input to the sensory apparatus, but rather is actively constructed and reconstructed 
through continual interchange between the individual and his 
physical and social environment. Cognitive, emotive, decision-making, and instrumental motor energy are also required 
to drive the system. Bach of these subsystems contributes to
29


## Страница 31

the structuring and operation of the others. Put another way, 
theory and practice work hand in hand.
3. 
Consequently, the classical philosophical approaches 
to epistemology and ontology mentioned above are seriously 
incomplete, focusing as each does on only one or two links 
and transformations of the total system. They have moved along 
parts of the sequence from information inputs from an assumed 
(or studiously denied) external world to the sensory apparatus, to its transformation into sensations, sense data, or 
percepts, and from thence to mental phenomena, concepts, ideas, 
images, or language and logical symbols. Typically, one or 
two of these transforms is taken as the "ultimate" or "primitive*1 raw data of knowledge, which is then to be verified 
either through internal 11 intuitive*1 operations or by some reductive mapping against objects and relations of the external 
world.
Such a model leads us to conclude, among other things, 
that it is at least midleading to claim that knowledge derives only from sense data from the external world* that, since 
information is inherently relation (a mapping between knower 
and known), it becomes meaningless to ask what the "real 
world" is like apart from a knower; and that to know the external world "only" through its effects on our senses is not 
at all a block or ultimate deficiency to "direct'* knowledge 
of the "real" external world. But most important for social 
science, we find that the individual is not simply a passive 
receiver and recorder of incoming signals and sense data, but 
actively contributes information along with the external signals by way of his external knowledge set, which constitutes 
one term of the relation giving meaning to information that 
would otherwise be nothing but noise. In addition to external 
signals, information is added to the total epistemological
30

system by (1) the basic physical structure of the nervous 
system; (2) by the on-going feedback from each phase of the 
transaction of the organism with its environment, whereby the 
latter is partly a matter of selection and construction from 
sampled cues relative to needs and goals; and (3) by the sociocultural process, including language and other symboling, 
in which the individual and his information-processing activities are constantly embedded.

В биологии в последние десятилетия происходит глубокая концептуальная эволюция. Классическая биология - дарвинизм, генетика и 
молекулярная биология - 
привела к представлению о том, что основные жизненные процессы, присущие организму, развертываются на 
уровне клетки и ниже, а основные процессы эволюции - на уровне менделевской популяции и вида. При этом жизненные процессы, характерные для клетки и многоклеточного организма, обычно считаются характерными для жизни вообще, процесс эволюции видов отождествляется с эволюцией живой природы, а филетическое дерево видов - с общей схемой живой природы. Такую точку зрения можно 
назвать 
организмои видоцентризмом.
Между тем комплекс современных экологических дисциплин постепенно ведет к существенно иному биологичекому миропониманию. Его 
характерные черты заключаются в следующем. Полагают, что идея организмои видоцентризма уже не отвечает современному биологическому знанию; все биологические системы от доклеточных объектов до 
биосферы играют в механизме живой природы функционально разную 
роль, но они в одинаковой мере характеризуют явление жизни, которое 
оказалось более стратифицированным, чем могло казаться ранее. В 
»том смысле жизненные процессы на уровне организма характерны

31


## Страница 33

только для него, но не для сообщества; точно так же процессы на 
уровне сообщества характерны только для него, но не для организма.
Другая важнейшая черта формирующегося биологического миропонимания - представление о том, что общая схема живой природы без 
экологических систем неполна, а поэтому классическая схема, т.е. 
филетическое дерево видов, и схема экологических систем должны 
быть 
каким-то образом объединены. В равной мере неполны и представления об эволюции живой природы, если они основываются на данных и концепциях одной лишь биологии организма и вида. Таким образом, центром внимания все более становятся не организм и вид, как 
было прежде, а живая природа как единое целое. Такую точку зрения 
можно было бы назвать био—космоцентрической. Но правильнее сказать, 
что она вообще отрицает идею центризма какой-либо одной системы.
В этом смысле она полицентрична.
Формирующееся новое биологическое миропонимание значительно 
более отвечает практическим задачам человечества, вступившего в 
период самого тесного общения с биосферой, чем концепция организмои видоцентризма. Оно отвечает также изменившимся потребностям 
биологического образования, которое лавинообразно насыщается новым 
материалом. Этот материал может быть компактно "уложен" в сознании лишь на основе достаточно всеобъемлющей теоретической матрицы. 
Матрица, созданная в классической биологии, при всей ее ценности, не 
охватывает большого объема новых знаний и, на наш взгляд, не отражает развивающейся в настоящее время ориентации на живую природу 
как целостную систему.
Развитие нового взгляда на живую природу совпало с развитием 
в биологии системного подхода. Можно указать следующие тенденции 
этого подхода, ведущие к созданию теории биосистем: эволюция задач 
биологии от частных к общим, эволюция языка биологии от конкретного 
к абстрактному, эволюция количественного подхода к организованным 
биологическим структурам на основе абстрактного языка.
В эволюции главных биологических задач в морфологии, физиологии, 
генетике, экологии наблюдается общая тенденция, закономерно ведущая от несистемного к типично системному пониманию и изображению 
объектов. Эта тенденция заключается в переходе от конкретных описательных задач (и, соответственно, изображений объектов) ко все 
более общим задачам, т. е. к созданию моделей возрастающей степени 
абстрактности.
В организменной биологии исходная задача заключалась в феноменологическом описании огромного количества животных и растений, по 
необходимости такое описание было совершенно конкретным, как это 
и требовалось для определителей. Постепенно классификация организмов привела к идее видов и других систематических единиц. С другой 
стороны, в морфологии начались поиски типов и "плана" строения. 
Примерно то же происходит и в физиологии, где создаются все более 
общие модели физиологических функций.
Аналогичное движение ( но гораздо более быстрое) совершается в 
экологии, в особенности 
в биоценологии. От описания отдельных биоценозов переходят к созданию обобщенных моделей. Многие из них
32


## Страница 34

остаются 
довольно конкретными, но построение математических моделей означает появление весьма абстрактных изображений исследуемых 
объектов.
Сказанное относится не только к структурно-функциональным, но и 
к 
июлюционным изображениям организма и других объектов.
Эволюции биологических задач отвечает эволюция языка биологии, 
и принципиально она одинакова как в сфере изучения организма и вида, так и в сфере изучения экологических макросистем, хотя есть и 
различия, определяемые спецификой объектов. Общее направление этой 
эволюции - от языка признаков к языку абстрактных понятий ( все 
более общих, вплоть до понятий, относимых ко всем живым системам).
Язык признаков был наиболее типичен и отвечал задачам оргапизменной биологии в период описания разнообразия организмов и построения их классификации. По мере выдвижения задач все более обобщенного 
характера, кроме типичной терминологии признаков, стала 
развиваться терминология элементов (гомологи, метамеры, полимеры 
и т.п.) с сопутствующими им численными абстрактными характеристиками. Изменчивость животных и растений также в значительной мере 
стала описываться в терминах изменения количества 
гомологов ( например, полимеризация и олигомеризация гомологов). И в физиологии 
организмов язык признаков постепенно изменяется, пополняясь терминологией межэлементных связей и функций переноса.
В еще большей мере сказанное о биологическом языке относится 
к экологии, объекты которой и их структура практически сразу стали 
описываться в категориях количества элементов (организмов разных 
видов, организмов одного вида), их разнообразия, доминирования и 
т. п. С другой стороны, стали формироваться описания структуры экологических систем, выраженные языком межэлементных связей (в сообществе это межорганизменные связи, в первую очередь ( трофические). 
В ряде случаев описания такого рода начали делаться с помощью функций переноса вещества, энергии или 
информации, 
т. е. на том языке, 
что и описания организмов и технических 
систем.
В настоящее время анализ структур биологических систем и их эволюции не опирается на единую количественную основу. Вместе с тем 
эволюция задач и языка биологии сопровождается расширением и углублением возможностей единого количественного описания морфологических и функциональных структур как организмов, так и экологических 
объектов. Это же относится и к количественному описанию эволюции 
данных структур.

H A R N E S S IN G M A N ’S SENSITIVITIES FOR T H E B E N E F IT OF M A N K IN D
It is no historical accident that man has become restless 
during the decade in which he first put his foot on the moon*
This feat in outer space has stirred a yearning within the 
realm of what has been called "the inner space of man*s mind 
and heart" to free himself from the outmoded ideas and attitudes which in the past have narrowed his vision, anesthetized 
his social conscience, and slowed his social progress*
Nor is it any accident that, accompanying this inward 
yearning for broader vistas and more wholesome and human living, new patterns of thought, action, and interaction are being designed to liberate man from these conceptual and attituf
dinal shackles*
Magic Carpets for Mental Space Travel 
And this is where gene red systems theory comes in* This 
approach to structuring events and knowledge offers the promise of equipping the mind with the conceptual vehicles which 
the heart is demanding* The metaphorical, analogical, and isomorphic images of general systems theory might be viewed as 
"magic carpets” for exploring the universe of the mind and 
heart* While the heart selects the destination and indirectly 
provides the lift and propulsion, the mind provides the calculations and judgments which guide the intuitive space ships 
toward their destination* (The general nature of this type of 
analogy to mental space travel is outlined in an address^*)
"The Indispensable Minimum of Knowledge"
For decades the need has been growing for major surgery 
on our educational system so as to adapt it to the demands of 
the future* As was implied above, the coming of general systems 
theory now does much to make this basic surgery possible* Within
34


## Страница 36

 the context of this basic need and of the emerging capabilities for simplifying and restructuring knowledge, the education committee (Task Force on General Systems Education) of 
the Society for General Systems Research was developed in 1968 
to generate promotional strategies, developmental models, innovative materials, and other prerequisites to success in simplifying, synthesizing, and vitalizing educational curriculums 
in all fields at all levels*
These new patterns of organization of knowledge are centered around a common vocabulary (metalanguage) and a supporting master model (meta-model) portraying the basic elements 
of all of the sciences, humanities, and arts* With these "meta-tools” we expect to be able to develop operational versions 
of what Kenneth Boulding has called "the indispensable minimum 
of knowledge”* Without this minimum or essential knowledge, we 
suspect that the infoimation explosion might prove to be a bane instead of a blessing* With this minimum we will have in 
future years a general conceptual orientation to determine 
what specific bits of information are needed in specific situations and also a means of organizing and adapting this information to our needs* Without reducing the strategic importance 
of concrete, matter-of-fact information in any practical situation, this body of more or les universal knowledge will help 
identify the figurative "needle" of relevant and important 
facts contained in the constantly mushrooming "haystack" of 
accumulated information*
Essentially the approach is a versatile vehicle for simplifying our mental conceptions of the world ir which we live and 
for integrating these images into our human value systems* It 
involves bringing into the realm of our conscious minds of the 
common-sense principles we have used intuitively -.and simply - 
since childhood*

## Страница 37

One of the informal missions of the Task Force is to fan 
the spark of sensitivity to sicietal needs as a basis for a 
concerted global effort to improve human communications in this 
era of information overload and overspecialization* The broad, 
simple, integrated patterns of knowledge being developed provide a basis for simplifying and blending the best of research 
and problem-solving techniques developed within the last three 
decades in the aerospace and defense industries*
Through this means we aspire to help people of all ages in 
all walks of life improve their ability to navigate on their 
own in the relatively strange, dynamic, interdependent, and 
complex intellectual, social, and physical worlds of their tra-vels during the next several decades*
To experience the 0°y and power of discovery while exploring some of the wonders of science in one,s surroundings is 
believed to be one of the greatest lessons of science and culture. To be able to reflect on these new insights and to apply 
them to one's own problems are believed to be significant means 
of reinforcing both the method and spirit of science* Nothing 
short of total education for a total environment can be expected to equip the whole man for the challenges of space-age democracy*
In a word, general systems practitioners specialize in 
simplicity through synthesis. Because of the motivational and 
perceptual power of reasoning by analogy, metaphor, and isomorphy, explorations of the entire intellectual world can take on an 
aura of adventure in the wonderland of simplicity* This emphasis on simplicity through synthesis is believed to be "the wave 
of the future" - if there is to be a democratic, humanitarian 
future*
Designing and Achieving a Democratic Global Future
Because of the critical need to devote special attention
to the task of designing a democratic, global future, tentative 
36


## Страница 38

 plants are being made at the SCSC Center for I-D Creativity, in cooperation with the Consortium on Systems Education and 
the SGSR Task Force and the World Future Society, for a program 
of studies which will provide students with opportunities to 
design and explore alternative futures for mankind.
Within this context, five questions dominate our efforts:
1. Will there be a (long-range) future for man?
' 2. If so, Can we design a future which will highlight the 
humanity of man (rather than blunder into a regimented, unbridled technology)?
If so, Can we design and implement an effective strategy for translating this design into an action program which will 
enlist respect and active involvement at the grass-roots level 
throughout the world?
4. If so, How can we best redesign our entire educational 
system (curriculums, administration, facilities, hardware, software, (etc.) so as to enable man to move "from man to mankind?"
5* How can we best redesign and simplify our structures of 
knowledge so as to be able to develop the educational capabilities which in turn will lead toward the desired humanitarian 
future?
This ambitious program of education is being labeled "societal or social architecture". Social architecture at its best 
consists of dreaming about the best world which has a chance of 
coming to be, identifying its landmarks, blueprinting its structure, and developing operational strategies for reaching it.
This approach is a resourceful one with as much emphasis on creating the will as on charting the way. It begins with the world 
which needs to be, which might be. and, indeed, can be. It then 
inventories resources are available and compares them with those
which are needed. The strategic step then is to develop an operational strategy and supporting system of tactics for transforming


## Страница 39

 the available resources into the needed resources* The task 
of achieving the future ideal is then reduced to the relatively 
routine one of applying the strategic blueprint while the strategists move on to dream about the next quantum leap*
Designing a desirable future is one thing* Achieving some 
reasonable semblance of that future is quite another* For that 
reason, these some cooperating agencies are now formulating a 
longer-range project, using the social architecture program of 
studies as but a stepping stone into this broader venture* Called 
operation man to mankind, this three-decade, global venture is 
designed to accelerate the transformation of provincial, selfcentered man into a united, organic, global mankind* Based at 
the SCSC Center for Interdisciplinary Creativity, the project 
focuses on the need for grassroots involvement in designing the 
global democracy of tomorrow* The strategy is to simplify and 
integrate knowledge and curriculum patterns so as to make possible 
the involvement of laymen everywhere in the task of creating alternative designs for the global democracy of the 21st Century.
The procedure is to utilize a simple metalanguage and supporting metasorroastsr^model to identify and develop "the indispensable minimum of knowledge", which in turn is being used to 
open minds, broaden perspectives, and blueprint pathways to tomorrow. One of the meta^models being developed is essentially 
a generalized extension of the economizing (means-ends) process 
which is used to identify, classify, and systematize common denominators of thought in all the sciences, humanities, and arts.
The project was officially launched at the International 
Workshop, Pathways from Man to Mankind, held at the Sheraton 
Motor Inn in Windsor, Connecticut, April 29-30, 1971*
The keynote speaker, Dr* Glenn T*Seaborg, charter member of 
the World Future Society Board and, at that time, Chairman of the 
Atomic Energy Commission, said: "The next thirty-odd years will

1*0* The role of topological concepts in abstract mathematical models of general systems has received little attention in 
the literature to date, considering the importance of such concepts in the representation of many basic systems properties. 
Mathematical theories of general systems proposed so far contain 
essentially only the set-theoretic structure of the underlying 
specific systems as part of the general theory. Thus, when such 
a general system model is restricted to subsume a class of specific systems containing topological structure as a fundamental 
property, it becomes necessary to introduce additional structure into the restricted model to adequately represent the specific class at hand. Such a theory lacks the degree of completeness 
at the abstract general level required of a general systems theory, In particular, the theory omits a construct - namely topological structure - which represents an "essential trait" 1^ of 
the class of specific systems which are presumably to be subsumed by the general system model.
To motivate the need for topological structure in formal 
mathematical models of general systems, we follow an inductive 
approach by giving examples of classes of specific systems where 
topological concepts play a fundamental role in the system description, The topological structure will be seen to be that of the 
"classical" topological space. The motivation is then pursued by 
determining a natural topological structure for the set of input 
functions for a general system model due to A,W,Wymore-^, This 
is achieved by a formed deductive approach and yields the interesting result that the natural structure is not the classical
S ) 
2 )
topological spaoe^y but a generalized closure space y which we
have denoted the H-Appert space.

## Страница 42

We will now discuss briefly examples of specific system 
models in which the use of topological concepts is basic to the 
representation of fundamental system properties (See ' for a 
more complete account, including an extensive bibliography). By 
representing a broad class of such specific systems, we attempt 
to motivate the need for topological structure as part of the 
abstract framework of any mathematical theory of general systems.
2.0. Out discussion begins with applications in the field 
of engineering. Although the literature of applications in this 
field is very extensive, we will discuss work3 which are either 
of a fundamental nature, or are representative 0f efforts in 
general. We consider the two areas of dynamical physical systems 
and control theory.
2.1. In the model of dynamical systems based upon the concept of a state space^ and used a0 the basis for studying stability properties of linear dynamical physical systems, a dynamical system is defined^ as a mathematical structure involving 
topological spaces which are the state space, the set
of admissible inputs, the system output functions, and a state 
transition function.
Topological considerations which arise for the subclass of 
this class of specific systems corresponding to linear, real, 
finite dimensional, continuous time systems include the preservation of stability properties, which requires as a necessary 
condition, topological equivalence. In these cases, the topologies are those induced by the Euclidean norm on finite dimensional spaces and the resultant topologies induced on the relevant 
product spaces.
A fundamental concept in dynamical physical systems and 
one which has also been the attention of significant study centers around the notion of approximation and its derivatives: 
optimality or optimization. Inherent in the concept of optimization
41


## Страница 43

 in the context of dynamical systems is that of approximation, which in turn, at the fundamental level, can be rigorously 
described by the constructs utilized in the theory of topological 
spaces.
2#2# In Control Theory, generalization has been presented 
by Halkin yielding a unified axiomatic and topological analysis 
of control problems for a very large class of systems, including 
systems described by differential and difference equations#
2#3# The work on functional systems by M#Brilliant is particularly relevant to the objectives of this study since, in 
addition to the formal introduction of a topological space, he 
has also motivated and explicitly determined the relevant open 
sets which define the topology# Thus it serves as an instructive 
example of the manner in which such structure may be introduced 
into a geven set-theoretic model of a specific class of systems 
to rigorously represent intuitive notions of approximation and 
continuity#
The topological consideration which arise within this class 
of systerns stem from the desire to address the following question: given the set of input functions, what structure defined 
over this set will account for the intuitive notion of approximation in the two cases: One, in the sense of generating an 
approximating table of values, i#e# the approximation of all input-output pairs for the system by a finite collection of such 
pairs; and two, in the sense of approximation of an arbitrary 
member of a subclass of functional system by a member of a "simpler" subclass of functional systems within the class#
2#4# The systems of interest in computer science are the 
discrete or countable systems which serve as models for digital 
computation#
In the work by M#Arbib, the need for topological structure 
appears even in this case where the discrete nature of the systerns
42


## Страница 44

 might suggest that such structure would have ancillary relevance* In particular, with reference to the need for representing continuity, Arbib suggests that the problem may be posed 
as that of determining a relevant topology for a discrete set 
which is not the discrete topology*^; that is, a definition of 
continuity in a form such that it is meaningful for finite automata* Pollowing this motivation, Arbib has introduced the 
concept of a tolerance and from that, the notion of continuity 
of functions mapping one tolerance space into another*
Another example of the role of topological concepts in 
automata systems where countable sets are involved is the study 
of the relationships between continuity and realizability of 
sequence transformations* The principal topological property of 
interest in this area is that of compactness, which is utilized 
strongly in determining conditions sufficient to realize a specific class of sequence transformations in terms of the class 
of logic nets having no feedback* In this case, such topological 
structure also yields a system synthesis criterion - a fundamental consideration in any system theory*
2*5* Kinship studies in anthropological systems have been 
cast into mathematical models where the basic structuring is 
accomplished via topological spaces* For example, the question 
of whether various separations of kinsmen by semantic features 
correspond to separations in the structure of the semantic space 
can be investigated by first defining a topology on a set of 
kin terms (using geneological relations), and then comparing the 
properties of the topology to the properties derived from the 
semantic analysis* The specific property of the topology is that 
of the connectedness of the associated set and the resultant 
system of components*
2*6* The role of topological structures in Economics is of 
course firmly established, especially if one considers the large
43


## Страница 45

emphasis in contemporary economics on optimization. In such cases, the topological structure is not usually singled out in 
the system description since the relevant sets are the real line 
or Cartesian products of subsets of the real line, where the 
topology is the usual topology on the real line, or the induced 
product topology. The need for topological structure to account 
for optimization, approximation, stability and optimal control 
is well documented in the literature of mathematical economics.
In the area of optimal resource allocation models, the extensive 
use of topological structures, namely linear topological spaces, 
is exhibited in the work on programming in linear spaces.
2.7. The study of perception and the concept of structure in 
social psychology involves the construction of models wherein 
the topological concepts of connectedness, boundary* and continuity have made an intuitive entry. Some particular applications 
of these'concepts appear in the gestalt theory of perception, 
where the correspondence between stimulus patterns and the associated brain patterns is viewed as being topological in the sense 
that the correspondence preserves relationships of "betweennesses” 
and "adjacences” in a perceived figure, rather than exact sizes, 
angles or shapes. A derivative of this getalt approach, which 
eliminates physiological considerations and instead focuses on 
the phenomenological concept, called the field, is the field-theory of behavior with special emphasis directed towards motivation 
and goal-directed action described in terms of the topological 
properties of the field.
2.8. The use of topological concepts and methods in contemporary theoretical physics is comprehensive and profound, reflecting the position of the field as the model par excellence of a 
mature mathematical science. Thus we choose here to simply mention some recent work concerning the concept of elementary length 
as applied in theoretical studies of quantum mechanical particle
44


## Страница 46

scattering. Preliminary efforts in this area have utilized a 
"natural topology" to account for effects of an elementary length 
where such a length is considered as a fundamental number Jl such 
that all length measurements are integer multiples of A . In an 
attempt to explain some recent anamolous scattering results, the 
concept of a topological potential due to the existence of an 
elementary length has been proposed* The question of which class 
of topologies, when placed on the particle coordinate system, is 
consistent with certain natural requirements of an elementary length has also been addressed* It has been demonstrated that there 
is essentially only one such class, thereby also placing on a
firm basis the ad-hoc topology initially assumed in earlier 
attempts to explain the scattering anomaly*
3*0. Turning from consideration of the use of topological 
spaces in specific systems models, we now briefly outline some 
results of our study of the topological structure intrinsic to 
the formal model of a general system due to Wymore^* In particular, we explore a relationship between the work of P*C* Hammer 
on extended topology2' a n d the general system model of Wymore* 
Although a formal systems model can, in an ad-hoc manner, 
incorporate a topological structure by simply postulating that 
the sets of interest carry a classical topology, that is that 
they are to be a-priori considered topological spaces, we have 
taken the approach that it is of more fundamental value to enquire 
into the existence of a natural topology as determined by the 
intrinsic algebraic and set-theoretic properties of the structure 
itself* If such a topology were suggested, subsequent considerations would explore its system-theoretic implications concerning 
approximation, continuity, etc* The pursuit of this approach with 
respect to the general systems model due to Wymore has yielded 
some interesting results concerning a topological structure intrinsically determined by the t h e o r y ' i n particular we have
45


## Страница 47

been able to show that: (i) within the framework of the elements 
of a mathematical theory of general systems as developed by Wymore, the method of generating admissible sets of input functions 
suggests naturally a function, the Admissible Set Operator, which 
satisfies, in general, not the well-known Kuratowaki closure axioms, but those closure axioms introduced by Hammer in his work on 
extended topology: (ii) as a consequence of (i), the topology 
suggested by the theory, i.e. the topology associated with the 
Admissible Set Operator, is not, in general, a classical topology, 
but the extended topology of Hammer: (iii) closed subspaces of 
the Hammer topological space represent a topological extensiongeneralized closure spaces, or H-Appert spaces - of the set of 
input functions for a Wymore general system.
In conclusion, in this work we have, motivated the position 
that topological structure should be an intrinsic component of 
any abstract theory of general systems - the method used was 
inductive via a brief description of the role of such structure 
in a broad class of specific systems; an examination of the 
weakest (most general) closure properties possessed by the set- 
- theoretic structure representing one approach*^ to an abstract 
model of general systems led to a topological structure of greater generality than the (classical) topological space generated 
by the usual Kuratowaki closure axioms. Since the work reported 
here generates many new problems, much remains to be done to 
attain the ultimate goal of introducing a relevant topological 
structure in abstract models of general systems.

Б.С. Фл е й шм а н (СССР)
СИСТЕМНЫЕ ЗАДАЧИ СОВРЕМЕННОЙ НАУКИ
В теории сложных систем известны два подхода к построению моделей. Первый состоит в приближении модели к действительности путем 
учета все большего числа качества оригинала. Трудность этого подхода связана с так называемым проклятием размерности [1]. Второй 
подход состоит в приближении оригинала к наиболее оптимальному, как 
правило, наиболее простому прототипу среди возможных (когда изучается не то, что есть, а то, что могло бы 
быть [2 ] ). Здесь возникают "телеологические* трудности. Но именно такое рассмотрение 
простых моделей реально сложных систем не снижает эффективности 
выводов о предельных возможностях последних, если считать идеальными те качества реальной системы, которые игнорируются в модели. 
На базе этих представлений сейчас формируется теория потенциальной 
эффективности сложных систем [3 ]. В порядке нарастания сложности 
поведения это - автоматические, решающие, 
самоорганизующиеся, 
предвидящие и превращающиеся системы.
47


## Страница 49

Модели и законы названной теории сложных систем аналогичны аксиомам и теоремам математики. Однако между ними имеется принципиальная разница - кроме логических требований математики, теория 
потенциальной эффективности требует еще и осуществимости своих 
объектов. Это требование труднее формализовать, чем логические, хотя на естественнонаучном уровне оно формализуется без труда.
Разветвленные методы анализа систем, в том числе математическое 
программирование и исследование операций, находят сейчас повсеместное использование. Это скорее расчетные методы, чем собственно теории (сколько систем, столько и их моделей).
Говоря о теории сложных систем, мы имеем в виду системную интерпретацию теорий надежности, информации, управления и других разделов теоретической кибернетики, составляющих содержание теории потенциальной эффективности сложных систем [ 3]. Самостоятельное использование результатов перечисленных разделов теоретической кибернетики общеизвестно. Сейчас наступает период развития и использования 
собственно системных подходов, связанных с "двойными сечениями" 
системы, например, по живучести.
Этому способствует наметившееся в последнее время новое взаимопроникновение техники и биологии. С одной стороны, это инженерный 
подход в управлении природными ресурсами, проблемы реконструкции 
природы и предотвращения экологического кризиса. С другой стороны, 
влияние биологии на технику связано с новым развитием высшего раздела бионики - психоморфизма, эволюционного моделирования [4] и 
рефлексивных игр [5].
Большинство возникающих здесь задач можно отнести к трем основным проблемам. Пусть 
система А , имеющая структуру ( А ), отвечает на входное воздействие среды В выходной реакцией А:

46


## Страница 50

 диаграммы "сложность - устойчивость" для животных на основе 
теории живучести [ 3] . Важнейшей проблемой здесь оказывается проблема устойчивости.
Иерархическому ряду усложняющихся систем соответствует ряд 
определяющих видов их устойчивости: прочность, сбалансированность, 
надежность, живучесть, структурная устойчивость и т. д. При этом 
наблюдается тенденция к все более разнородному составу элементов 
системы при постоянстве ее структуры. Развивается теория структурной устойчивости и генезиса сложных систем на основе оценок вероятности связности случайных графов.
Как уже упоминалось, имеются два подхода в построении моделей: 
первый - в пространстве действительности, второй - в пространстве 
возможностей. Каждому из них соответствуют специфические методы 
исследований (см^ таблицу 2 )

методологии ( [ 5 ] и др.) связаны с отождествлением-потенциального и

нашему мнению, вряд ли актуальна утонченная математическая формализация понятий теории сложных систем до накопления достаточного 
количества положительных результатов. Для их получения комбинаторный анализ возможностей имеет такое же значение, какое в свое время имел для физики анализ бесконечно малых величин.
